\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)-v_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}-v_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}+v_{0}\frac{v_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+v_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}}{v_{0}}

by
Equation

2

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)

•

[Dep]
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{0}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v_{1}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
.
We have that:

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}}[{\sigma}]\mid\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}}[{\sigma-v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}-v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}+v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid\{{r_{0}+v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}+v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Gamma_{0}

where:

v=\frac{v_{0}\cdot\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}{r_{0}}

If
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
’s net worth is unaffected
(note that the value of minted tokens in
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
’s wallet is
preserved by deposits, by Lemma
6
3
).
Otherwise, if
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}={\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
, then:

\displaystyle W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma^{\prime})

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)-v_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}-v_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}+vP_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)-v_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}-v_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}+v\frac{r_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+r_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}

by
Equation

2

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)-v_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}-v_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}+\frac{v_{0}}{r_{0}}\Big(r_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+r_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)-v_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}+\frac{v_{0}}{r_{0}}r_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)

since
r_{1}v_{0}=r_{0}v_{1}

•

[Swap]
.
This case cannot happen, since we are assuming

\mathit{type}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}})\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}
.

•

[Rdm]
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}})
.
We have that:

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}}[{\sigma}]\mid\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}}[{\sigma+v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}+v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}-v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid\{{r_{0}-v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Gamma_{0}

where:

v_{0}=\frac{v\cdot r_{0}}{s}\qquad v_{1}=\frac{v\cdot r_{1}}{s}\qquad s=\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

If
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
’s net worth is unaffected
(note that the value of minted tokens in
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
’s wallet is
preserved by redeems, by Lemma
6
3
).
Otherwise, if
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{B}}}={\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
, then:

\displaystyle W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma^{\prime})

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)+v_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+v_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-vP_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)+v_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+v_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-\frac{v\cdot r_{0}}{s}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}-\frac{v\cdot r_{1}}{s}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)+\frac{v\cdot r_{0}}{s}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+\frac{v\cdot r_{1}}{s}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-\frac{v\cdot r_{0}}{s}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}-\frac{v\cdot r_{1}}{s}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}

\displaystyle=W_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}(\Gamma)

We now prove
item

2
,
i.e. that the
global
net worth is preserved by
any
transactions.
First, we recall from
section 3

the definition of global net worth.
Let:

\Gamma\;=\;{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{1}}}[{\sigma_{1}}]\mid\cdots\mid{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{n}}}[{\sigma_{n}}]\mid\{{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}}\}\mid\cdots\mid\{{r_{k}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{k}}}},{r^{\prime}_{k}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{k}}}}\}

Then, the global net worth of
\Gamma
is:

W(\Gamma)\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{i}}}}(\Gamma)

We have the following cases:

•

[Dep0]
,
[Dep]
,
[Rdm]
.
These rules affect the token reserves in AMMs,
which do not contribute to the global net worth,
and the balances of users, which we know to be preserved.
Therefore, the global net worth is preserved.

•

[Swap]
.
Let
{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({v},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

be the fired transaction.
We have that:

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\;\mid\;\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\;\mid\;\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma-v:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}+v^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}]\;\mid\;\{{r_{0}+v:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-v^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\;\mid\;\Gamma_{0}

The global net worth in
\Gamma^{\prime}
can be computed in terms of
the global net worth in
\Gamma
,
by removing the value of the
v:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}
paid by
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
to the AMM,
adding the value of the
v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}
obtained by
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}

through the swap,
and then adding the difference between the value of the minted tokens
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
and in
\Gamma
, i.e.:

\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}P_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}-\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}P_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

By Lemma
5
, we have that

\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}=\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}
.
Therefore:

\displaystyle W(\Gamma^{\prime})

\displaystyle=W(\Gamma)-vP{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+v^{\prime}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}+\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}\big(P_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}-P_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}\big)

\displaystyle=W(\Gamma)-vP{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+v^{\prime}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}\cdot\Big(\dfrac{r_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+r_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}+vP{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}-v^{\prime}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}-\dfrac{r_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}+r_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=W(\Gamma)

∎

Proof of Lemma
8

Direct consequence of Lemma
7
1

and of the hypothesis that
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
does not hold minted tokens in
\Gamma^{\prime}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9

Let
\Gamma^{0}=\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta^{0}

be a reachable state.
We define below a procedure to construct a sequence of transitions:

\Gamma^{0}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}}\cdots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{n}}}\Gamma^{n}\qquad\text{where }\;\Gamma^{n}=\{{r_{0}^{i}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}^{i}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta^{n}

By Lemma
4
, we have that

r_{0}^{i}>0
,
r_{1}^{i}>0
,
and
\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{i}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}>0
for all
i
.
At step
i
:

(1)

Let
x=r_{0}^{i}-r^{\prime}_{0}

be the amount of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}
that users must redeem from the AMM,
and let:

v=\frac{x}{r_{0}^{i}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{i}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

(2)

if there exists some
{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\in\Gamma^{i}
such that

\sigma(\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\})\geq v
, then

{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
can fire
{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}})
,
obtaining, for some
r^{\prime}_{1}\leq r_{1}
:

\displaystyle\{{r_{0}^{i}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}^{i}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta^{i}\xrightarrow{}\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Big\{{r_{0}^{i}-v\frac{r_{0}^{i}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{i}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}},\,{r^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\Big\}\mid\cdots

\displaystyle=\{{r^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\cdots

(3)

otherwise, pick an
{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\in\Gamma^{i}
such that

\sigma(\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\})=v^{\prime}\geq 0
, fire

{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v^{\prime}:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}})
.

Note that the procedure always terminates:
since
\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{i}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}>0
for all
i
,
either step
(2)
or
(3)
can be performed;
further, the number of performed transactions is bounded
by the number of users, which is finite.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10

Assume that

\Gamma\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}}\Gamma_{0}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}}\Gamma_{01}
.
We have the following exhaustive cases
on the type of the transactions
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}
:

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{0}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{0}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v^{\prime}_{0}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}}})
.

(a)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{1}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{1}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{v^{\prime}_{1}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
.
Both transactions are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}
,
so we are in case
1
of the statement.
If
\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}}\}\neq\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}\}
,
then the thesis is straightforward, since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}

operate on different AMMs.
Otherwise,
let:

\begin{array}[]{llll}a_{0}=1+\tfrac{v_{0}}{r_{0}}&m_{0}=\tfrac{v_{0}}{r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}&a_{01}=1+\tfrac{v_{1}}{a_{0}r_{0}}&m_{01}=\tfrac{v_{1}}{a_{0}r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma_{0}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}\\[10.0pt]
a_{1}=1+\tfrac{v_{1}}{r_{0}}&m_{1}=\tfrac{v_{1}}{r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}&a_{10}=1+\tfrac{v_{0}}{a_{1}r_{0}}&m_{10}=\tfrac{v_{0}}{a_{1}r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}\end{array}

We have that:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\mid\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}}\;

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma-v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}-v^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}+m_{0}:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid\{{a_{0}r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{a_{0}r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}}\;

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma-(v_{0}+v_{1}):{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}-(v^{\prime}_{0}+v^{\prime}_{1}):{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}+(m_{0}+m_{01}):\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid

\displaystyle\{{a_{01}a_{0}r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{a_{01}a_{0}r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

Inverting the two transactions, we obtain:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\mid\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}}\;

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma-v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}-v^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}+m_{1}:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid\{{a_{1}r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{a_{1}r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}}\;

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma_{1}-(v_{0}+v_{1}):{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}-(v^{\prime}_{0}+v^{\prime}_{1}):{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}+(m_{1}+m_{10}):\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid

\displaystyle\{{a_{10}a_{1}r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{a_{10}a_{1}r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

We have that
a_{01}a_{0}=a_{10}a_{1}
, since:

\displaystyle a_{10}a_{1}

\displaystyle=\big(1+\tfrac{v_{0}}{a_{1}r_{0}}\big)a_{1}=\frac{a_{1}r_{0}+v_{0}}{r_{0}}=\frac{\big(1+\tfrac{v_{1}}{r_{0}}\big)r_{0}+v_{0}}{r_{0}}=\frac{r_{0}+v_{0}+v_{1}}{r_{0}}

\displaystyle a_{01}a_{0}

\displaystyle=\big(1+\tfrac{v_{1}}{a_{0}r_{0}}\big)a_{0}=\frac{a_{0}r_{0}+v_{1}}{r_{0}}=\frac{\big(1+\tfrac{v_{0}}{r_{0}}\big)r_{0}+v_{1}}{r_{0}}=\frac{r_{0}+v_{0}+v_{1}}{r_{0}}

Furthermore, we have that
m_{0}+m_{01}=m_{1}+m_{10}
, since:

\displaystyle m_{10}+m_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{0}v_{1}+a_{1}r_{0}v_{1}+r_{0}v_{0}}{a_{1}r_{0}^{2}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{0}v_{1}+v_{1}(r_{0}+v_{1})+r_{0}v_{0}}{(r_{0}+v_{1})r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(v_{0}+v_{1})(r_{0}+v_{1})}{(r_{0}+v_{1})r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}\;=\;\frac{v_{0}+v_{1}}{r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

\displaystyle m_{01}+m_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{0}v_{1}+a_{0}r_{0}v_{0}+r_{0}v_{1}}{a_{0}r_{0}^{2}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{0}v_{1}+v_{0}(r_{0}+v_{0})+r_{0}v_{1}}{(r_{0}+v_{0})r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(v_{0}+v_{1})(r_{0}+v_{0})}{(r_{0}+v_{0})r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}\;=\;\frac{v_{0}+v_{1}}{r_{0}}\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}

Summing up, we have shown that
\Gamma_{01}=\Gamma_{10}
.

(b)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{1}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({v_{1}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}})
.
Then, we are in case
1
of the statement,
with
\mathit{tok}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}})
disjoint from
\mathit{tok}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}})
.
The thesis is straightforward by analysis of the rules.

(c)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{1}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v_{1}:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}}\}})
.
There are two subcases.
If we are in case
1
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}
operate on different AMMs,
and so the thesis is straightforward.
Otherwise, if we are in case
2
of the statement,
by hypothesis we know that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}
is enabled in
\Gamma
,
leading to a state
\Gamma_{10}
.
If
\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}}\}\neq\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}\}
,
then the thesis is straightforward.
Otherwise, the proof is done by computing the states

\Gamma_{01}
and
\Gamma_{10}
and showing they are equal,
similarly to what we have done in case (
1a
).

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{0}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v_{0}:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}}}\}})
.

(a)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{1}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{1}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{v^{\prime}_{1}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}})
.
Symmetric to case (
1c
).

(b)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{1}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({v_{1}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}})
.
Then, we are in case
1
of the statement,
where
\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}\}
and
\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}}\}

are disjoint.
Then, the thesis is straightforward.

(c)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{1}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v_{1}:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}}\}})
.
Then, we are in case
1
of the statement.
If
\mathit{tok}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}})
is disjoint from
\mathit{tok}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}})
,
then the thesis is straightforward.
Otherwise, note that the tokens paid by the AMM in response of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}

only depend on the ratio between the amounts
of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}}
initially held by the AMM,
which are constrained to preserve the ratio.

(3)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{0}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({v_{0}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}}})
.
The only case not covered by the previous items is when

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{1}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({v_{1}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}})
.
Then, we are in case
1
of the statement,
where
\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{1}}}\}
and
\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}}\}

are disjoint.
The thesis is straightforward. ∎

Proof of Theorem
11

For item
1
,
there are two cases, depending on whether
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}
is fired through
rule
[Dep0]
or
[Dep]
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}
is fired through rule
[Dep]
,
let
\Gamma=\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta
.
We have that:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=\{{r_{0}+v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}+v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle r_{1}v_{0}=r_{0}v_{1}

(13)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\{{(r_{0}+v_{0})+v^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{(r_{1}+v_{1})+v^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle(r_{1}+v_{1})v^{\prime}_{0}=(r_{0}+v_{0})v^{\prime}_{1}

(14)

We must just check that the premises for firing

{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{0}+v^{\prime}_{0}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v^{\prime}_{1}+v^{\prime}_{1}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

are satisfied:

\displaystyle r_{1}(v_{0}+v^{\prime}_{0})

\displaystyle=r_{1}v_{0}+r_{1}v^{\prime}_{0}

\displaystyle=r_{0}v_{1}+r_{1}v^{\prime}_{0}

by (
13
)

\displaystyle=r_{0}v_{1}+r_{1}\Big(\frac{r_{0}+v_{0}}{r_{1}+v_{1}}\Big)v^{\prime}_{1}

by (
14
)

\displaystyle=r_{0}v_{1}+r_{1}\frac{r_{0}}{r_{1}}v^{\prime}_{1}

by (
11
)

\displaystyle=r_{0}(v_{1}+v^{\prime}_{1})

The case where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{0}}
is fired through rule
[Dep0]
is similar:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=\{{v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\{{v_{0}+v^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v_{1}+v^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle v_{1}v^{\prime}_{0}=v_{0}v^{\prime}_{1}

The premises of
[Dep0]
when firing

{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{0}+v^{\prime}_{0}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v^{\prime}_{1}+v^{\prime}_{1}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

are trivially satisfied, hence the thesis follows.

For item
2
,
let
\Gamma=\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau}}=\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}
, and
let
s=\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau}}}
.
By rule
[Rdm]
, we have that:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=\{{r_{0}-v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle v_{i}=v\cdot\frac{r_{i}}{s}

(15)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\{{(r_{0}-v_{0})-v^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{(r_{1}-v_{1})-v^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle v^{\prime}_{i}=v^{\prime}\cdot\frac{r_{i}-v_{i}}{s-v}

(16)

Therefore, for
i\in\{0,1\}
, we have that:

\displaystyle r_{i}-v_{i}-v^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=r_{i}-v\cdot\frac{r_{i}}{s}-v^{\prime}\cdot\frac{r_{i}-v\cdot\frac{r_{i}}{s}}{s-v}

by (
15
), (
16
)

\displaystyle=r_{i}-v\cdot\frac{r_{i}(s-v)}{s(s-v)}-v^{\prime}\cdot\frac{sr_{i}-v\cdot r_{i}}{s(s-v)}

\displaystyle=r_{i}-\frac{vr_{i}(s-v)+v^{\prime}(sr_{i}-vr_{i})}{s(s-v)}

\displaystyle=r_{i}-\frac{vr_{i}(s-v)+v^{\prime}r_{i}(s-v)}{s(s-v)}

\displaystyle=r_{i}-(v+v^{\prime})\cdot\frac{r_{i}}{s}

from which the thesis follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
12

By cases on the rule used to deduce
\Gamma\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}}\Gamma^{\prime}
.
The premise that
\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau}}}=0
implies
\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau}}}=0

excludes the case
[Dep0]
, so we have two cases:

•

[Dep]
.
We have that

{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{0}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v_{1}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
,

\Gamma={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\mid\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta
,
and:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma-v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}-v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}+v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid\{{r_{0}+v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}+v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma^{\prime}}]\mid\{{r^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

where
v=\frac{v_{i}}{r_{i}}\cdot s
, with

s=\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}})
.
We have that:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma^{\prime}+v^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}+v^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}-v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid\{{r^{\prime}_{0}-v^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}-v^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta\;=\;\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

where, for
i\in\{0,1\}
and
s^{\prime}=\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}=s+v
:

\displaystyle v^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=v\cdot\frac{r^{\prime}_{i}}{s^{\prime}}=v\cdot\frac{r_{i}+v_{i}}{s+v}=\Big(\frac{v_{i}}{r_{i}}\cdot s\Big)\cdot\frac{r_{i}+v_{i}}{s+\big(\frac{v_{i}}{r_{i}}\cdot s\big)}=\frac{v_{i}s(r_{i}+v_{i})}{r_{i}s+v_{i}s}=v_{i}

Since
v_{i}=v^{\prime}_{i}
for
i\in\{0,1\}
, we conclude that

\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma
.

•

[Rdm]
.
We have that

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}})
,

\Gamma={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\mid\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta
,
and:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma+v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}+v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}-v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid\{{r_{0}-v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma^{\prime}}]\mid\{{r^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta

where
v_{i}=v\cdot\frac{r_{i}}{s}
,
for
i\in\{0,1\}
and
s=\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{0}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v_{1}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
.
We have that:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma^{\prime}-v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}-v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}+v^{\prime}:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}]\mid\{{r^{\prime}_{0}+v_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}+v_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta\;=\;\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

where
v^{\prime}=\frac{v_{i}}{r^{\prime}_{i}}\cdot s^{\prime}
,
with
s^{\prime}=\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}=s-v
.
We have that:

\displaystyle v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{i}}{r^{\prime}_{i}}\cdot s^{\prime}=\frac{v\cdot\frac{r_{i}}{s}}{r_{i}-v\cdot\frac{r_{i}}{s}}\cdot(s-v)=\frac{v\cdot r_{i}}{sr_{i}-vr_{i}}\cdot(s-v)=\frac{v}{s-v}\cdot(s-v)=v

Since
v^{\prime}=v
, we conclude that
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
13

The condition
\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}>0

ensures that
\Gamma
contains an AMM for the pair
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}
.
The premise
\sigma({\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}})\geq x
ensures that
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}

has enough units of the input token
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}
.
Output-boundedness implies the premise

x\cdot\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})<r_{1}

of
[Swap]
.

Proof of Theorem
15

Let
\Gamma=\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta
.
We have that:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=\{{r_{0}+x_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-y_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle y_{0}=x_{0}\cdot\mathit{SX}({x_{0},r_{0},r_{1}})

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\{{r_{0}+x_{0}+x_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-y_{0}-y_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle y_{1}=x_{1}\cdot\mathit{SX}({x_{1},r_{0}+x_{0},r_{1}-y_{0}})

Since
\mathit{SX}
is additive, we have that:

\mathit{SX}({x_{0}+x_{1},r_{0},r_{1}})\;=\;\frac{y_{0}+y_{1}}{x_{0}+x_{1}}

Therefore, rule
[Swap]
gives the thesis:

\Gamma\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({x_{0}+x_{1}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})}\{{r_{0}+x_{0}+x_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-(y_{0}+y_{1}):{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta_{1}

Proof of Lemma
16

Since
\mathit{SX}
is output-bounded, then by Lemma
13
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0}+x_{1})
are enabled in
\Gamma
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})
is enabled in
\Gamma^{\prime}
.
Let:

\alpha=\mathit{SX}({x_{0},r_{0},r_{1}})\qquad\beta=\mathit{SX}({x_{1},r_{0}+x_{0},r_{1}-\alpha x_{0}})

By additivity of
\mathit{SX}
(Definition
5.3
), we have that:

\gamma=\mathit{SX}({x_{0}+x_{1},r_{0},r_{1}})=\frac{\alpha x_{0}+\beta x_{1}}{x_{0}+x_{1}}

(17)

Therefore:

\displaystyle\hskip-12.0pt\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0}+x_{1})})-\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})})

\displaystyle=\gamma(x_{0}+x_{1})P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-(x_{0}+x_{1})P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}-\alpha x_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}+x_{0}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}

(Lemma
1
)

\displaystyle=\big((\gamma(x_{0}+x_{1})-\alpha x_{0}\big)P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-x_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}

\displaystyle=\big(\alpha x_{0}+\beta x_{1}-\alpha x_{0})\big)P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-x_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}

(Equation
17
)

\displaystyle=\beta x_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-x_{1}P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})})

(Lemma
1
)

∎

Proof of Theorem
17

Let

\Gamma=\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta
, and
let
y=x\cdot\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})
.
By the
[Swap]
rule, there exists
\Delta^{\prime}
such that:

\Gamma^{\prime}\;=\;\{{r_{0}+x:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-y:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta^{\prime}

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({y},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}})
, and
let
x^{\prime}=y\cdot\mathit{SX}({y,r_{1}-y,r_{0}+x})
.
For some
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, we have:

\Gamma^{\prime}\;\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}}\;\{{r_{0}+x-x^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-y+y:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta^{\prime\prime}

By reversibility of the swap rate, we have that:

\frac{y}{x}=\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\implies\mathit{SX}({y,r_{1}-y,r_{0}+x})=\frac{x}{y}

from which we obtain that:

x^{\prime}\;=\;y\cdot\mathit{SX}({y,r_{1}-y,r_{0}+x})\;=\;y\cdot\frac{x}{y}\;=\;x

from which we obtain the thesis.
∎

Proof of Lemma
18

Straightforward from the definition of gain and
from Theorem
17
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
19

Let
\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\in\Gamma
,

\{{r^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\in\Gamma^{\prime}
,
and let
a=\nicefrac{{r^{\prime}_{0}}}{{r_{0}}}
.
We have that:

\displaystyle X_{\Gamma}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

\displaystyle=\lim_{x\rightarrow 0}\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})

by

by
Equation

Proof of Lemma
20

For item
1
,
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}({v_{0}}:{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{v_{1}:}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
.
By rule
[Dep]
,

r^{\prime}_{i}=r_{i}+v_{i}
for
i\in\{0,1\}
,
with
r_{0}v_{1}=r_{1}v_{0}
.
By Lemma
6
1
,

\nicefrac{{r_{0}+v_{0}}}{{r_{1}+v_{1}}}=\nicefrac{{r_{0}}}{{r_{1}}}
.
Then:

r_{0}+v_{0}=\frac{r_{1}+v_{1}}{r_{1}}r_{0}=a\,r_{0}\qquad r_{1}+v_{1}=\frac{r_{1}+v_{1}}{r_{1}}r_{1}=a\,r_{1}

Therefore:

\displaystyle\mathit{SX}({x,r^{\prime}_{0},r^{\prime}_{1}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({x,ar_{0},ar_{1}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({\tfrac{x}{a},r_{0},r_{1}})

(homogeneity)

\displaystyle>\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})

(strict monotonicity,
a>1\implies\tfrac{x}{a}<x
)

The thesis

\Delta X_{\Gamma}({x,{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})>\Delta X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}({x,{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

follows from this inequality and Lemma
19
.

For item
2
,
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}({v:\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}})
.
By rule
[Rdm]
, for
i\in\{0,1\}
:

r^{\prime}_{i}\;=\;r_{i}-v_{i}\;=\;r_{i}-v\frac{r_{i}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\;=\;a\,r_{i}

Therefore:

\displaystyle\mathit{SX}({x,r^{\prime}_{0},r^{\prime}_{1}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({x,ar_{0},ar_{1}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({\tfrac{x}{a},r_{0},r_{1}})

(homogeneity)

\displaystyle<\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})

(strict monotonicity,
a<1\implies\tfrac{x}{a}<x
)

The thesis

\Delta X_{\Gamma}({x,{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})<\Delta X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}({x,{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

follows from this inequality and Lemma
19
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
22

For output-boundedness,
let
x>0
and
r_{0},r_{1}>0
.
We have that:

\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})=\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}+x}<\frac{r_{1}}{x}

For monotonicity,
Let
x^{\prime}\leq x
,
r^{\prime}_{0}\leq r_{0}
and
r_{1}\leq r^{\prime}_{1}
.
We have that:

\mathit{SX}({x^{\prime},r^{\prime}_{0},r^{\prime}_{1}})\;=\;\frac{r^{\prime}_{1}}{r^{\prime}_{0}+x^{\prime}}\;\geq\;\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}+x}\;=\;\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})

The proof for strict monotonicity is similar.

For additivity,
by Definition
2.2
we have that:

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})=\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}+x}

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({y,r_{0}+x,r_{1}-\alpha x})=\frac{r_{1}-\alpha x}{r_{0}+x+y}=\frac{r_{0}r_{1}}{(r_{0}+x)(r_{0}+x+y)}

Therefore:

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha x+\beta y}{x+y}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x+y}\Big(\frac{r_{1}x}{r_{0}+x}+\frac{r_{0}r_{1}y}{(r_{0}+x)(r_{0}+x+y)}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x+y}\frac{r_{0}r_{1}x+r_{1}x^{2}+r_{1}xy+r_{0}r_{1}y}{(r_{0}+x)(r_{0}+x+y)}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{1}(r_{0}+x)(x+y)}{(x+y)(r_{0}+x)(r_{0}+x+y)}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}+x+y}

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({x+y,r_{0},r_{1}})

For reversibility,
let
\alpha=\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})
.
By Definition
2.2
, we have that:

\mathit{SX}({\alpha x,r_{1}-\alpha x,r_{0}+x})\;=\;\frac{r_{0}+x}{(r_{1}-\alpha x)+\alpha x}\;=\;\frac{r_{0}+x}{r_{1}}\;=\;\Big(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}+x}\Big)^{-1}=\frac{1}{\alpha}

For homogeneity, we have that:

\mathit{SX}({ax,ar_{0},ar_{1}})\;=\;\frac{ar_{1}}{ar_{0}+ax}\;=\;\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}+x}\;=\;\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})

The computations of the internal exchange rate and of the slippage
are straightforward.
∎

Proof of Theorem
23

Output-boundedness, monotonicity and homogeneity are straightforward.
For additivity,
by Definition
23
we have that:

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})=\frac{r_{1}}{x}\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{r_{0}}{r_{0}+x}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({y,r_{0}+x,r_{1}-\alpha x})=\frac{r_{1}-\alpha x}{y}\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{r_{0}+x}{r_{0}+x+y}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}\bigg)

Therefore:

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha x+\beta y}{x+y}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x+y}\Bigg(\alpha x+(r_{1}-\alpha x)\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{r_{0}+x}{r_{0}+x+y}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x+y}\Bigg(r_{1}-r_{1}\Big(\frac{r_{0}+x}{r_{0}+x+y}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}+r_{1}\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{r_{0}}{r_{0}+x}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}\bigg)\Big(\frac{r_{0}+x}{r_{0}+x+y}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x+y}\bigg(r_{1}-r_{1}\Big(\frac{r_{0}}{r_{0}+x}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}\Big(\frac{r_{0}+x}{r_{0}+x+y}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{1}}{x+y}\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{r_{0}}{r_{0}+x+y}\Big)^{\frac{w_{0}}{w_{1}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathit{SX}({x+y,r_{0},r_{1}})

∎

Proofs for Section

Proof of Lemma
24

Assume that
\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\geq X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
.
Let
\alpha(z)=\mathit{SX}({z,r_{1},r_{0}})
.
We have that:

\displaystyle\mathit{SX}({y,r_{1},r_{0}})

\displaystyle<\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\mathit{SX}({z,r_{1},r_{0}})

(strict monotonicity)

\displaystyle=\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\mathit{SX}({\alpha(z)\cdot z,r_{0}-\alpha(z)\cdot z,r_{1}+z})}

(reversibility)

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})}

(strict monotonicity)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})}

(hypothesis)

\displaystyle=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}})

(def. of
X
)

∎

where in the second application of strict monotonicity, we have exploited the
(asymptotic) inequalities
\alpha(z)\cdot z<x

(where
\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\alpha(z)\cdot z=0
follows from the existence
of the internal exchange rate),

r_{0}-\alpha(z)\cdot z<r_{0}
, and

r_{1}+z>r_{1}
.

25

y>0
.

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{d}}(x)})>0
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{d}}(x)

, and so

\mathit{SX}({x,r_{d},r_{1-d}})>X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{d}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1-d}}}})
.

it follows that

\mathit{SX}({y,r_{1-d},r_{d}})<X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1-d}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{d}}}})

\sigma{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1-d}}}}\geq y

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1-d}}(y)

.
By using again Lemma
2
, concluding that

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1-d}}(y)})<0
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
26

Let
x_{0}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
be as in the hypotheses, i.e.:

\Gamma\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})}\Gamma^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma^{\prime}}]\mid\{{r_{0}+x_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-\alpha x_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta\qquad\text{where }\begin{array}[]{l}\alpha=\mathit{SX}({x_{0},r_{0},r_{1}})\\
X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})\end{array}

We have two cases, depending on whether
x>x_{0}
or
x<x_{0}
.

•

If
x>x_{0}
, let
x_{1}>0
be such that
x=x_{0}+x_{1}
.
Since
\mathit{SX}
is output-bounded and additive,
then by Lemma
16
:

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x)})\;=\;\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})})+\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})})

(18)

We have that:

\displaystyle\mathit{SX}({x_{1},r_{0}+x_{0},r_{1}-\alpha x_{0}})

\displaystyle<\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\mathit{SX}({z,r_{0}+x_{0},r_{1}-\alpha x_{0}})

(strict monotonicity)

\displaystyle=X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

def.
X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

(hypothesis)

Then, by Lemma
2
we obtain

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})})<0
.
By
Equation

18
,
we conclude that

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x)})<\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})})
.

•

If
x<x_{0}
, let
x_{1}>0
be such that
x_{0}=x+x_{1}
.
Since
\mathit{SX}
is output-bounded, then by Lemma
13
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x)
are enabled in
\Gamma
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})
is enabled in the state
\Gamma_{1}

reached after performing
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})
, i.e.:

\Gamma\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x)}\Gamma_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})}\Gamma^{\prime}

Since
\mathit{SX}
is output-bounded and additive,
then by Lemma
16
:

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})})\;=\;\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x)})+\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma_{1}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})})

Since
\mathit{SX}
is reversible, then by Theorem
17
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})
has an inverse, which has the form

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}(x_{1})={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({y_{1}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}})

for some
y_{1}>0
.
Then, by Lemma
18
,

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma_{1}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})})=-\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}(y_{1})})
,
therefore:

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})})\;=\;\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x)})-\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}(y_{1})})

(19)

We have that:

\displaystyle\mathit{SX}({y_{1},r_{1}-\alpha x_{0},r_{0}+x_{0}})

\displaystyle<\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\mathit{SX}({z,r_{1}-\alpha x_{0},r_{0}+x_{0}})

(strict monotonicity)

\displaystyle=X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}})

def.
X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})}

(Equation (
8
))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})}

(hypothesis)

\displaystyle=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}})

(def.
X
)

Then, by Lemma
2
we obtain

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}^{-1}(y_{1})})<0
.
By Equation (
19
),
we conclude that

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x)})<\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})})
.

For uniqueness, by contradiction assume that there exists
x_{1}\neq x_{0}

satisfying
Equation

9
.
Then, it should be

\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{1})})>\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}(x_{0})})

— contradiction.
∎

Proof of Lemma
27

Let
\Gamma\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}}\Gamma^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma^{\prime}}]\mid\{{r_{0}+x_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}-x_{0}\cdot\mathit{SX}({x_{0},r_{0},r_{1}}):{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}
.
We have that:

\displaystyle X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{1}-x_{0}\cdot\mathit{SX}({x_{0},r_{0},r_{1}})}{r_{0}+x_{0}}

by Theorem
22

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{1}-x_{0}\cdot\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}+x_{0}}}{r_{0}+x_{0}}

by Definition
2.2

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{0}r_{1}}{(r_{0}+x_{0})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{0}r_{1}}{\frac{P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}}{P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}}r_{0}r_{1}}

by
Equation

10

\displaystyle=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

by
Equation

3

The thesis follows from Theorem
26
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
28

Let:

\Gamma={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\mid\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta\;\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}}}}\;\Gamma^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma^{\prime}}]\mid\{{r^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta^{\prime}

The hypothesis

\mathit{wal}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}}}})={\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}\neq\mathit{wal}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}}}})

means that the user who performs the deposit is
not

{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
,
hence the deposit does not affect the number of minted tokens
in
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
’s wallet.
Then:

\displaystyle\hskip-12.0pt\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf dep}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}}}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}}}})

\displaystyle=x\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r^{\prime}_{0},r^{\prime}_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

(Lemma
1
)

\displaystyle>x\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

(Lemma
20
1
)

\displaystyle=x\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\sigma\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

(\sigma^{\prime}\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}=\sigma\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\})

\displaystyle>x\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\sigma\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

(\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}>\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}}}})

∎

Proof of Theorem
29

Let
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{d}
be as in the statement.
By rule
[Dep]
,

r^{\prime}_{i}=r_{i}+v_{i}
for
i\in\{0,1\}
.
By Lemma
6
1
, we have that

\nicefrac{{r_{0}+v_{0}}}{{r_{1}+v_{1}}}=\nicefrac{{r_{0}}}{{r_{1}}}
.
Then:

r^{\prime}_{0}=r_{0}+v_{0}=\frac{r_{1}+v_{1}}{r_{1}}r_{0}=a\,r_{0}\qquad r^{\prime}_{1}=r_{1}+v_{1}=\frac{r_{1}+v_{1}}{r_{1}}r_{1}=a\,r_{1}\qquad\text{where $a=\frac{r_{1}+v_{1}}{r_{1}}$}

For item (
1
),
assume that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\lambda}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({x},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

is a solution to the arbitrage game in
\Gamma
.
By Theorem
26
, it must be:

X_{\Gamma_{s}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})\qquad\text{ where }\Gamma\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\lambda}}}\Gamma_{s}

(20)

Let
x^{\prime}=ax
,
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({x^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
,
and let
\Gamma_{d}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}}}\Gamma_{ds}
.
We have that:

\displaystyle\hskip-12.0ptX_{\Gamma_{ds}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

\displaystyle=\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\mathit{SX}({z,r^{\prime}_{0}+x^{\prime},r^{\prime}_{1}-x^{\prime}\cdot\mathit{SX}({x^{\prime},r^{\prime}_{0},r^{\prime}_{1}})})

\displaystyle=\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\mathit{SX}({z,ar_{0}+ax,ar_{1}-ax\cdot\mathit{SX}({ax,ar_{0},ar_{1}})})

\displaystyle=\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\mathit{SX}({z,ar_{0}+ax,ar_{1}-ax\cdot\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})})

(homogeneity)

\displaystyle=\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\mathit{SX}({z,r_{0}+x,r_{1}-x\cdot\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})})

(homogeneity)

\displaystyle=X_{\Gamma_{s}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

(def.
X_{\Gamma_{s}}
)

\displaystyle=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

(Equation (
20
))

Therefore, Theorem
26
implies that

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}}
is a solution to the arbitrage game in
\Gamma_{d}
.
We compute the gain of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}}
in
\Gamma_{d}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma_{d}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}}})

\displaystyle=x^{\prime}\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x^{\prime},r^{\prime}_{0},r^{\prime}_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)

\displaystyle=ax\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({ax,ar_{0},ar_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)

\displaystyle=ax\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)

(homogeneity)

\displaystyle=a\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}})

For item (
2
),
assume that
\varepsilon
is a solution to the arbitrage game in
\Gamma
.
By contradiction, assume that

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\lambda_{d}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}({x^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

is a solution in
\Gamma_{d}
.
By Theorem
26
, it must be:

X_{\Gamma_{ds}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})

(21)

The chain of equations above shows that

X_{\Gamma_{ds}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})=X_{\Gamma_{s}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
.
By Equation (
21
), this implies that

X_{\Gamma_{ds}}({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})=X({{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}})
.
Hence, by Theorem
26
,
\varepsilon

cannot be a solution to the arbitrage game in
\Gamma

— contradiction.
∎

Proof of Theorem
30

Let:

\Gamma={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma}]\mid\{{r_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta\;\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}}}}\;\Gamma^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}[{\sigma^{\prime}}]\mid\{{r^{\prime}_{0}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{r^{\prime}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}\mid\Delta^{\prime}

The hypothesis

\mathit{wal}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}}}})={\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}\neq\mathit{wal}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}}}})

means that the user who performs the redeem is
not

{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
,
hence the redeem does not affect the number of minted tokens
in
{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}
’s wallet.
Then:

\displaystyle\hskip-12.0pt\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf rdm}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}}}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma^{\prime}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}}}})

\displaystyle=x\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r^{\prime}_{0},r^{\prime}_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

(Lemma
1
)

\displaystyle<x\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

(Lemma
20
2
)

\displaystyle=x\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\sigma\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

(\sigma^{\prime}\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}=\sigma\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\})

\displaystyle<x\cdot\big(\mathit{SX}({x,r_{0},r_{1}})\,P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}-P{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}}\big)\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\sigma\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\Big)

(\mathit{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}<\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{G}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}{\sf{A}}}}({\Gamma},{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf swap}}}}})

∎

Proof of Theorem
31

Let
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{d}
be as in the statement.
By rule
[Rdm]
, it must be, for
i\in\{0,1\}
:

r^{\prime}_{i}\;=\;r_{i}-v_{i}\;=\;r_{i}-v\mathit{RX}^{i}_{\Gamma}({\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}})\;=\;r_{i}-v\frac{r_{i}}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}\;=\;ar_{i}\qquad\text{where }a=1-\frac{v}{\mathit{S}_{\Gamma}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{0}}}},{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\tau_{1}}}}\}}}

The rest of the proof follows exactly
that of Theorem
29
.
∎
